( It could possibly get some substitute , albeit much weaker , " guarantees " from Europe . ) <p> In the South African context , it is actually incorrect to speak of a ' debate on ' this issue in mainstream media and corporate circles , because it is largely a one-way street . Russia and Putin are vehemently denounced . Putin is simply diabolical . He embodies the resurrection of Adolf Hitler and is , therefore , far right , a fascist , racist and similar evils , or simply irrational @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the aggressor who " barbarically " invaded Ukraine without cause . Contrary to the so-called " rules-based international order " as determined by international law , the roaring Russian bear is trampling on the " national sovereignty " and " territorial integrity " of another state -- the completely innocent Ukraine under the leadership of the heroic President Volodymyr Zelensky . Russia 's operation expresses its unbridled expansionist drive , according to which Ukraine is just the first victim , after which the insatiable bear will devour the rest of Europe . Moreover , the Russian bear 's bloodthirsty army is killing civilians indiscriminately . <p> During the first phase of the current conflict , it was also confidently predicted that the Russian public would rise up against Putin and topple his government . It was also expected that Russia would lose the war . <p> Everything was essentially unfounded . There is no evidence that Russia is a rising power intent on overtaking Central and Western Europe . Nor is Russia capable of that . It is much weaker than the former Soviet Union , of which Russia was @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ are also not consistent with the Russian military wantonly wiping out civilians . According to the UN High Commissioner for Human Rights , civilian casualties from February 24 2022 , when the war began , to January 31 2025 , amounted to 12 605 deaths and 29 178 injuries . This is far fewer than the civilian casualties in Gaza during the recent conflict there in three times the period and far fewer than South Africa 's annual murder tally . <p> Now , leading forces in the Russian elite indeed view Russia as an empire -- in the footsteps of the Roman and Byzantine empires . According to this self-consciousness , Russia is not simply a nation-state . Quite rightly , Russia also perceives itself as the spearhead of a unique civilisation -- the ( Christian ) Orthodox civilisation -- and as such the protector of the Orthodox world that also extends far beyond the borders of Russia , namely in Southeast Europe and elsewhere . This empire -- and civilisation -- in addition to its national consciousness and its vast natural resources , makes Russia a formidable power @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ balanced and kept in check by counterpower in the best tradition of realpolitik . <p> However , there is an even more important fact that weighs heavily in the current circumstances , but is unfortunately largely overlooked . It is that Russia , because of its history , is suspicious , weary , and even somewhat fearful of dangers that threaten it , more particularly from its western flank . From its west , Russia has been struck several times by unprecedented disasters and misery . <p> In 1812 , disaster from the west came in the form of the devastation wrought by the Grand Arm ? e -- the imperial army of Napoleon Bonaparte . The Russians were forced to abandon Moscow , leaving their self-burnt city behind , which was occupied by Napoleon . Ultimately , Napoleon 's power was broken on his retreat to France , but the devastation of their country left an indelible mark on the minds of the Russian people . <p> Russia suffered great damage from the west again during World War I and had to cede large parts of its territory with @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Russia ( then part of the Soviet Union ) was again devastated from the west . Nazi Germany invaded Russia from 1941 onwards , inflicting enormous losses . <p> Towards the end of the war and after , the Russians swept across Eastern and Central Europe , creating a belt of communist vassal states around the Soviet Union . The Soviet Union thereby extended communist rule far west . <p> While this constituted an aggressive expansion of communism , there is also a defensive interpretation of this , which takes into account the Russian experience and fear of aggression from its west . <p> According to this , Russia wanted and did establish a cordon of security around itself to repel future threats far beyond its borders . To this end , it also established the Warsaw Military Pact with the now communist states to its west in 1955 , which stood in opposition to NATO , which was founded in 1949 after the Second World War . <p> After 1989 , this defensive Russian strategy collapsed . After the fall of the Berlin Wall , all these countries moved @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Warsaw Pact dissolved on July 1 , 1989 . With this , the entire Russian defence constellation that was supposed to protect it from threats from its west was gone . It was once again exposed to dangers from its western flank . <p> Russia 's vulnerability was exacerbated when the Soviet Union itself dissolved only a few months later . As a result , its buffer to its immediate west in the form of Ukraine , which had been part of the Soviet Union , was removed , leaving Russia in the same geopolitically vulnerable position as before World War I. <p> In these circumstances , it was of the utmost importance for Russia to secure its western border by redeveloping a sphere of influence in its western near-abroad . However , several former Warsaw Pact members -- the Czech Republic , Hungary , Slovakia , Poland and Romania -- understandably joined NATO in a desire to counter Russian influence . Former Soviet states , namely Lithuania , Latvia and Estonia , are also now part of NATO . <p> But not Ukraine . Russia could not tolerate Ukrainian @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ hundred kilometres from Moscow , in the heart of Russia . <p> In 1962 , the Soviet Union under Nikita Khrushchev cooperated with Fidel Castro to deploy Russian nuclear missiles in Cuba . Under the " rules-based international order , " it was perfectly within the sovereign rights of the Soviet Union and Cuba to do so , just as it was within the sovereign rights of the US and Turkey to deploy missiles in Turkey . <p> However , President John F Kennedy and his administration opposed the deployment of missiles in Cuba and were even prepared to go to war with the Soviet Union to prevent it . Kennedy 's military and diplomatic pressure was eventually so formidable that Khrushchev and Castro had to abandon their plans . <p> Were Kennedy and his men irrational or " crazy " to act this way ? Apparently not . In order to protect American security , they could not tolerate this kind of behaviour from Khrushchev and Castro . <p> But what about the much-vaunted " rules-based international order " ? There is in international relations a system of ( @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ interstate relations ( diplomatic immunity , extradition of suspected criminals , etc. ) international law is ordinarily followed . But when the core interests of states , especially great powers , are at stake , so-called international law becomes irrelevant . No government or head of state can be expected to compromise vital security interests in deference to international law norms . Not Kennedy in 1962 and not Putin in 2025 . <p> Putin sought to protect the security interests of Russia through the Minsk agreements of 2014 and 2015 . However , the agreements foundered , ultimately leaving him with no choice but to take military action , at least according to the Russian interpretation of events . <p> Furthermore , since 2014 , after the ( unconstitutional ) removal of its then pro-Russian government , Ukraine has had a regime that is dismissive of Russia and pro-NATO . Russia consequently ( re ) occupied the Crimean Peninsula in 2014 . In doing so , it regained this area , which had been under its control with a few short interruptions since 1783 , and thus also secured Russian @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Sea ) , an obvious geostrategic imperative for Russia . <p> Since 2014 , a low-level civil war has also been raging in the Donbass region of eastern Ukraine , which is inhabited by Russians , among others . One of the causes of this is the allegation that the Ukrainian government is violating the language and political rights of the Russian-speaking population . <p> At the same time , the pressure for Ukraine to join NATO grew , which naturally rekindled Russia 's old and understandable fears of threats from its west . Russia would clearly not allow this and consequently proceeded to its special military operation in 2022 . <p> The Russian interpretation of the circumstances is of course contested . There are also opposing views . It is also clear that Russia underestimated Ukraine 's determination to defend itself . Russia also miscalculated NATO 's willingness to support Ukraine with weapons and to -- as it is sometimes cynically put -- fight Russia to the last Ukrainian . <p> However , it is crystal clear that Putin has not acted irrationally ; neither is he " crazy @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ 's troubled history referred to above , Putin is currently as rational and calculating as Kennedy was in 1962 . <p> Similarly , there are no grounds for labelling Putin the unqualified aggressor or for the propaganda that Russia is planning to overwhelm Europe . <p> There is just as little basis for the rather childish insinuation that the Trump administration is now " in Putin 's pocket " . <p> The American government has apparently moved away from the policy of trying to impose the " values and ideals of liberal democracy " and " human rights " everywhere in the world and , with that in mind , engaging in unsavoury wars everywhere . <p> Instead , the US appears to be moving toward practicing realpolitik . To that end , it advances its interests . Accordingly , it will wage war when required but will prefer to establish working relationships and make agreements -- deals , as Trump would say . <p> Russia 's approach is essentially the same . It does not , like the former Soviet Union , seek to implement communism and wage wars @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and especially its security , it has demonstrated that it will go to war , but would rather enter into working relationships and conclude agreements -- strike deals . <p> The new American government understands this well and , therefore , understands that it can indeed reach an agreement with Russia . They can count on each other because they understand each other 's interests . <p> There is likely now a growing understanding by other governments that they must follow in the footsteps of Moscow and Washington . If anyone acts outside their interests , the opponent will call them to order -- even militarily if it comes to that . <p> The way in which America and Russia are now increasingly acting towards each other ( and will hopefully find each other ) encapsulates much of the logic of interstate relations over the past centuries . Cardinal Richelieu , Clemens Metternich , Otto von Bismarck , Lord Palmerston and many other of the greatest figures in political history have practised realpolitik along this path . It has been thoroughly recorded in the annals of history , including fascinatingly @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of political realism such as Hans Morgenthau , George Kennan and Kissinger himself had a thorough insight into this , as do present day analysts Jeffrey Sachs and the formidable John Mearsheimer . <p> Perhaps without Donald Trump realising it , his art of the deal similarly is largely in accordance with this realist style of international relations .

What are the implications for relations with SA ? How is SA to respond to this case in a series of incidents signalling growing tensions with Washington ? <p> On Friday , the US secretary of statetheir foreign minister Marco Rubio , shocked the diplomatic world when he announced Rasool had been " declared persona non-grata and no longer welcome in the US " . <p> Accusing Rasool of being anti-American and anti-Trump , and for engaging in race-baiting , Rubio broke protocols by announcing this on the X platform . Even the South African government was shocked because , normally , states d ? marche an ambassador they think did something wrong , which means summoning him to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ shocking decision has context . There have been strains in relations between SA and the US for some time . In the Nelson Mandela years , this was managed by high-level forum chaired by the vice-presidents , but that later fell away . So , difficulties of the recent period , such as during the 2021 US-led bombing of Libya that attracted the ire of SA , the duty to patch things up fell to ministers and ambassadors . <p> Relations reached another low after differences over the Russia and Ukraine war , with the US annoyed by Pretoria 's choice of non-alignment instead of aligning with the US support of Ukraine . Things reached a climax when the then US ambassador to SA openly accused SA of supplying military arms to Russia via Simon 's Town harbour . This angered South Africans , but to manage this , Pretoria decided to reach out to Washington diplomatically . <p> Since US President Donald Trump ascended to power in January , we 've seen his posts on X about " bad things " happening in SA , culminating in an executive @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ " in SA . It seemed the campaign by white right-wing forces led by the Solidarity movement and AfriForum , as well as the DA , from 2023 had reached the ears of the right-wing government . <p> It is worth noting that Trump 's administration is strongly pro-Israel and , therefore , secretly abhors SA 's strong stance in support of Palestinians , including its decision to haul Israel before the International Court of Justice . This alone made Rasool 's appointment as ambassador a cause for concern because of his strong position against Israel 's genocidal war on Gaza and the US connivance in this . It seems they have not warmed up to him since taking over power . <p> So , when Rasool commented on the US foreign policy , linking it to the white supremacy agenda globally , Washington drew a red line and declared him persona non-grata . Rasool had raised a logical point of view in the political debate about the growing global right-wing tendencies . But it was risky for him as an ambassador to the US to link this negative phenomenon @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ as an unfriendly but also hostile comment . He should have known the relations are under strain and that the new White House would waste no time taking advantage of his comment to escalate the tensions . <p> Now , SA has to think carefully about the type of personality and skill they send to the US as a replacement . It knows the White House and state department will scrutinise that nomination and reserve their right to reject it . So , a career diplomat -- trained to handle complex conditions based on diplomatic practice rather than political attitude -- might be what we need . Rasool 's error was unlikely to be committed by a senior career diplomat because they are used to calm diplomatic demeanour and are not easily drawn into political debates . They tend to stick to communicating policy rather than strong opinions . <p> Pretoria will most likely continue with its charm offensive by sending special envoys bringing friendly messages from President Cyril Ramaphosa , consistent with his calm approach to this saga . But these envoys too must be chosen with great care @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ deficit that has developed between the two countries . SA should , at least , maintain trade , investment and scientific co-operation at current levels . <p> Pretoria must out how to dissuade AfriForum and Solidarity from fanning the diplomatic fires even more . <p> And diplomats and political figures should watch what they say so as not to trigger further tensions . The criticism of the right-wing can be left to analysts and activists until relations recover . We should also avoid taking the bait that gets laid out by figures associated with the MAGA movement , including Elon Musk , as these may be designed to give them reasons to escalate tensions . <p> Prof Zondi is director of Institute for Pan African Thought and Conversation at University of Johannesburg .

@@122651616 <h> OPINION <h> UCT opts for suicide <p> William Saunderson-Meyer <p> 21 March 2025 <p> William Saunderson-Meyer writes on council 's extraordinary refusal to modify its anti-Israel resolutions <p> JAUNDICED EYE <p> The Nelson Mandela legacy is finally depleted . The ' Madiba magic ' that opened international doors and wallets , and froze criticism in its tracks , is no more . Truth is , Donald Trump killed Nelson Mandela . <p> The halo of righteousness under which the African National Congress swaggered on the world stage since 1994 lies shattered . Already for some time , international obsequiousness has been wearing thin -- even the right-on Joe Biden administration became impatient with South Africa 's anti-Western posturing -- but it was who Trump finally drew the curtain on perhaps the longest-running illusion in modern history . <p> The American president 's freezing of billions in aid to South Africa , with more sanctions looming , has a simple moral . Actions have consequences . <p> The United States accepts that it is our sovereign right to consort with the scum of the earth , to defend and befriend @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ every turn . Equally , the US will make its own cost-benefit analyses . Rightly or wrongly , cleverly or foolishly , it has decided that based on present behaviour , it 's ending what has been an extraordinary display of generosity towards South Africa . <p> This is a sea change that the ANC and its supporters need to get to grips with so that they stop making fools of themselves and South Africa . It 's slow at happening . Instead , there has been self-pity and anger -- much rending of garments and gnashing of teeth -- directed at the USA , Trump , Elon Musk , Afrikaners , AfriForum and every other scapegoat imaginable . <p> --&gt; <p> However , there has been no sign of adapting foreign policies , modifying contentious legislation or moving away from the race-based criteria that have become ubiquitous in South Africa . After all , if the Expropriation and BELA acts are indeed so benign in intention as their defenders claim , what possible objection could there be to scrapping or rewriting a couple of clauses that endanger your entire @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ On the contrary , there has been a defiant doubling down by the ANC . Its leaders WILL fete Iranian despots . The Budget WILL find further millions for anti-Israeli court actions . Cyril Ramaphosa WILL co-author an article in Foreign Policy accusing Israel of systematic genocide and Trump of ethnic cleansing . <p> That 's not unexpected behaviour . The ANC is notoriously hide-bound ideologically and hence resistant to change . Even if Ramaphosa wanted to make major adaptations and compromises , it likely could only be done at the cost of again splitting the party . <p> But far more extraordinary than ANC 's predictable actions is when an autonomous university chooses self-immolation in preference to rescinding a motion that puts it at odds with most of its staff and alumni , as well as a critical mass of the donors who have poured billions into making it the leading academic institution on the African continent . That is what 's playing out at the University of Cape Town ( UCT ) . <p> --&gt; <p> As I wrote earlier this year ( * ) , anti-Israeli sentiment @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ array of non-governmental organisations and among many of the staff and administrators at our universities . It 's well-orchestrated and implacable , totally indifferent to the collateral damage caused locally , often to their own institutions . <p> As was detailed in High Court papers filed in February by historical studies Professor Adam Mendelsohn , who is contesting the legality of a UCT Council ( UCTC ) resolution enforcing an academic boycott of Israel , the financial damage caused to the university is potentially astronomical . Mendelsohn charges the UCTC executive committee with deliberately keeping Council members in the dark about the fact that it had been warned that hundreds of millions of rands earmarked by two donors for UCT would not be forthcoming if the academic boycott resolution was passed . <p> Mendelsohn -- since suspended as head of department by UCT , that great citadel of free thought , for challenging them in court -- also cited an internal UCT risk assessment that concluded that any ' reasonably significant ' cut in the university 's contract research funding would have ' catastrophic consequences ' . Using 2018 figures @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ losing almost 30% of its contract research revenue , R330m a year , while the ' worst case ' would be double that . <p> Since then , the Trump 2 era has dawned . February 's USAID freeze has been followed by whispers last week that the US National Institutes of Health ( NIH ) is similarly about to cut research ties and funding . This would be calamitous not only for UCT , the largest single recipient of NIH funding outside North America , but also for the Medical Research Council , which would lose 28% of its scientific budget . <p> --&gt; <p> From a report tabled by UCT Vice-Chancellor Professor Mosa Moshabela at a heated UCTC meeting this past weekend , during which he outlined the damage caused by the anti-Israel resolutions , it transpires that the true situation is far worse than anyone comprehended . On top of this , if the funding is ended , there is an additional indirect cost to UCT of R204 million . <p> Most of these NIH grants are linked to UCT 's flagship Institute of Infectious Disease and Molecular Medicine and focus on HIV and TB research . Their loss places at risk critical research infrastructure , UCT 's global research ranking , and the substantial state subsidy that the university gets for every journal article published . <p> The loss of intellectual capital would be staggering . UCT estimates that the salaries of 475 academic and research support staff depend wholly or in part on NIHI funding . There would be ' negative impacts ' on the university 's scientific visibility and its @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ membership of prestigious organisations like the International Association of Research Universities . <p> Columbia University has been hit by a US$400 million federal funding cut because of what the US government perceived as a failure to squelch antisemitism on campus . Moshabela reports that UCT was warned by the US Consulate that , similarly , its resolutions on Gaza risked ' triggering US sanctions targeted specifically at UCT ' . <p> The impact will be ' immense and sustained ' , said Moshabela . The Jewish community , which is proportionally over-represented in terms of endowment support , has stepped back . Donor income was down 42% in 2024 . Student debt jumped R227 million -- up almost 40% from R593 million to R820 million -- while student debt recovery sagged to 41% . <p> An @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the meeting , points out that Moshabela , supported by the Deputy VC for research and the Chief Financial Officer , was n't even that ambitious in his appeal to the UCTC . @@122755793 <h> OPINION <h> Fagan , Seekings and the battle for control of the narrative on Gaza <p> Leslie London <p> 24 March 2025 <p> Leslie London queries the publication of a confidential document related to internal disciplinary matter at UCT <p> Jeremy Seekings : Time for transparency <p> I was surprised to see Politicsweb publish what is clearly a confidential document related to an internal disciplinary matter at the University of Cape Town . Normally , one might just shrug this off as a blip on public opinion . But this publication of a legal recommendation for disciplinary action against Professor Jeremy Seekings takes place in the context of extraordinarily acrimonious contestation of ideas at the University of Cape Town ( UCT ) over what the University should be doing regarding the ongoing genocide of Palestinians in Gaza . <p> The Daily Maverick article of 16 October 2024 by Jeremy Seekings and Nicoli Nattrass , which is the basis for the investigation of disciplinary charges against Professor Seekings , attempted to portray a Senate decision in September 2024 not to recommend Professor Anton Fagan for emeritus status as discriminatory and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ decision was because of his position on the war on Gaza . That was not the case . <p> The questions raised in Senate were about the prima facie evidence that , as a result of student complaints about an exam he had set , he took early retirement from UCT because of an imminent prospect of a disciplinary hearing -- and that early retirement would be in both his and the Faculty 's best interest . He confirmed this in an interview with the South African Jewish report in July 2024 . There is no contesting that a disciplinary hearing for Professor Fagan was anticipated . <p> Faced with the idea that an academic could avoid a disciplinary hearing by taking early retirement but return as an emeritus staff member after retirement with never having to account for the complaint , the suggestion was put to Senate that the nomination was premature and we should await a formal report as promised by the University spokesperson . <p> The nominator declined to postpone the nomination and it was put to the vote . In the climate of uncertainty and the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Professor Fagan 's exam ( Seekings and Nattrass dismiss student experience as " hyperbolic " and as " wild claims " ) , the majority of Senate members declined to support his nomination . <p> There were too many questions unanswered about his retirement , and the absence of transparency about what action had been on the table or removed from the table by his departure . He was not refused emeritus status for anything other than unanswered questions about actions which were sufficiently serious to raise the prospect of a disciplinary hearing which his early retirement had avoided . <p> Seekings and Nattrass immediately turn that into grounds for accusation . As soon as the vote had concluded in Senate , one of the authors attacked those who raised concerns about Fagan 's nomination , saying that had Senate waited for the approval of a new policy on Emeritus appointments , the vote would have been fair . <p> --&gt; <p> Yet the article fails to acknowledge that the starting point of discussion in Senate was to ask the nominator to defer the nomination until we had a report @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the table . By that time , a new policy would have been in place . None of this is mentioned in the Seekings and Nattrass article because it does not serve the purpose of the narrative intended . <p> Quite frankly , many UCT academics are sick and tired of the mischief played out by colleagues using the media to mislead the public about what are matters that have been deeply debated in UCT structures . Smearing colleagues as practicing a cancel culture , being commissars , and creating a Manichean , self-righteous , censorious and adversarial culture is neither intellectually accurate nor reflective of any willingness to accept that their peers do n't agree with them that the University should tolerate special rules for some people . <p> But the narrative can not happen unless the press is thirsty to participate . So , I ask the editor of Politicsweb if you are willing to name the source that supplied you with this legal letter , a letter clearly not intended for the public . The selective release of information to the media in the context of a @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ be simply answered by resort to press freedom . It is directly undermining of due process in a quasi-legal matter , which should be allowed to run its course , unimpeded by external manipulation . <p> In this case , I think the public is entitled to know who gave you the letter so that it can judge for itself what is going on behind its release . That would be the acid test of whether Politicsweb believes its readership should have full information to assess an opinion piece . Public interest would be best served by transparency , please ! <p> --&gt; <p> Professor Leslie London is a Professor at UCT and a member of UCT Senate . In the interests of transparency , he declares that he proposed one of the two resolutions on Gaza adopted by Senate in 2024 and then approved by UCT 's council , now currently being challenged in court by another UCT academic .

We have taken necessary steps to secure our comuting environment and to keep your data safe . As per our T&amp;Cs we undertake to never sell or share any of your personal information to any 3rd party unless obliged to do so by law . Your information will solely be used for the personalisation of user experience as well as the storage of user settings and preferences . <p> This year 's report emphasises the urgency for brands to transcend superficial trends and contribute more meaningfully to culture , addressing the growing concern around cultural rot , characterised by an overload of repetitive and banal content . <p> TBWA says that as the attention economy dominates the Internet , people and brands have been turned into content machines , chasing relevance and dopamine hits , which ultimately leaves them feeling overstimulated , underwhelmed , always online , but disconnected . <p> According to the agency , this sentiment is reflected in the 2024 words of the year : @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ deterioration of online platforms as companies prioritise profits over user experience , and <p> " slop , " which is the flood of bizarre , low-quality AI-generated content dominating social media ( think : " shrimp Jesus " and " Trump Gaza " ) . <p> For brands in 2025 , the challenge is clear : stop following the algorithm and start creating meaning , says TBWASouth Africa 's CEO , Luca Gallarelli . <p> " The future belongs to those who build for impact , not trends , shaping culture rather than just reacting to it . The reality is that most marketers and journalists tracking ' culture ' are actually just following fleeting micro-trends ; it 's more about virality than values . Cultural movements have shrunk into brief , recycled moments , endlessly reposted but rarely practised in real life . This fixation on digital relevance has left society overstimulated yet disconnected , proving that the attention economy has reached a breaking point , " says Gallarelli . <p> This Edge recognises a shift in environmental action plans , as companies scale back sustainability targets and make @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ movement towards implementing affordable , scalable and readily available environmental solutions , like anti-Climate Change Conference gatherings that push for practical , inclusive and human-centric climate solutions that protect the masses over the privileged few . <p> Maturity Paradox <p> This Edge explores " fluid aging , " which sees the blurring lines between age and maturity . @@123015609 <p> The impending visit by Volodymyr Zelensky is an opportunity to demonstrate greater support and solidarity for Ukraine , the writer says . Illustration : KAREN MOOLMAN <p> Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelensky is slated to visit SA towards the end of April . While each day 's tumult in Trumpland seems to exceed the one before , Ukraine and SA have respectively provoked two of the most explosive recent Trump-ordered exits : the directive to Zelensky to leave the White House after the Oval Office shouting match , and the expulsion of SA ambassador to the US , Ebrahim Rasool . <p> Zelensky and Ramaphosa may have cause to rethink the SA visit . The sheer spite and conspiracy-inclined mindset of the Trump administration may have each speculating whether they can afford the ire such a visit might provoke from Trump . SABC senior international news editor Sophie Mokoena wrote recently on X that the number two demand of SA from the US is that we stop Zelensky 's visit to the country . <p> We should n't . And not from some desire to thumb our noses at Trump @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ that urge ) . We should roll out the carpet for Zelensky because revulsion at naked aggression and oppression , solidarity with the embattled people of his country , and respect for his wartime leadership of the country argue for such honour . But even if we do n't see it that way , we should do so in our shameless self-interest . <p> As is now trite , a torch has been put to what we have taken to be the rules-based international order , even if it has often only functioned as fac ? ade . Yet SA , even at its most ambitious approximation in any foreseeable future , can only hope to be a strategically located middle-power . Unless it pins its hopes on being a favoured flunkey of an existing or emerging power , SA 's security and sovereignty rest on preservation of , and reform to , multilateralism , the rules-based order and the norms and principles underpinning this system . <p> At the centre of this system is the principle of territorial sovereignty -- denoting that a state has exclusive authority and control @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , without external interference . It is also the principle most implicated , and its foundational status compromised , by Russia 's invasion of Ukraine . Any future " peace " for Ukraine that requires of the country that it cede parts of its territory , will be bought at the cost of that principle 's continued force . <p> Whatever might be required by SA 's previously declared position of neutrality vis- ? -vis Ukraine and Russia , this is the moment to declare its unambiguous support for Ukraine retaining the maximum possible geographical area and people within it . If nothing else , failure to do so chimes inconsistently with SA 's much scrutinised position on Israel and Gaza : if Russia can take territory and displace and dispossess its inhabitants , why exactly are Israel 's actions to be reviled ? <p> But more existentially , for the purposes of its own territorial integrity , dilution of the importance of this principle makes the demands of AfriForum , Solidarity , the Cape Independence group , and the more extreme flanks of the DA far more potent . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ ceded in satisfaction of Russia and under the pretext of nominal protection of Russian-speaking minorities in Ukraine , is a world susceptible to demands that chunks of SA are peeled off in satisfaction of aggrieved minorities ' demands for self-governance . <p> As the world resets -- and unless SA 's future trajectory is to be determined simply as flotsam of that tidal wave -- it will need to consciously define its placement . Unquestionably , our global standing is not what it once was . Our domestic record , and many of the actions we 've taken on the international stage , have in no way met the highest hopes and expectation of a democratic SA . <p> But degraded though our image may be , our record as a country that avoided civil war in the face of historic injustice , and more specifically a racial conflagration , and secured a constitution that guarantees a nation-state fashioned by constitutional norms and values , rather than immutable racial or ethnic ties , continues to exert a powerful influence on the global imagination . Especially now . <p> That is @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ reaction to SA 's International Court of Justice application regarding the situation in Gaza and an intensive targeting of the country for its role , despite the numerous other parties -- such as Belgium , Chile , Ireland , Mexico and Spain -- that have also joined the application . <p> And whatever one might feel about the wisdom of Rasool 's utterances that led to his expulsion , it 's hard to see that their veracity might be credibly contested . While we might only be a tawdry pastiche of what the " rainbow nation " ideal was meant to represent , the disproportionate amount of time and attention being spent vilifying SA by those in the US suggest SA is being made to function ideologically as even more of a failed state than it really is . <p> Whether because of demographic trends , as Rasool enumerated , or for other reasons , there appears to be a vested interest in holding out a narrative that white minorities face only grave peril and persecution within a multiracial nation state -- with SA being held up as the exemplar @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ only to provoke unnecessary enmity from the US . But there is no question we should be unapologetic in looking to frame ourselves as staunch upholders and reformers of the existing multilateral system . In this respect we will have to , quite clearly , be a counterpoint to what the US administration is intent on collapsing . <p> The impending visit by Zelensky , and the opportunity to demonstrate greater support and solidarity for Ukraine , allows us an opportunity to repair somewhat our damaged international standing . It may not win us any more affection from the US leadership , but it is a price worth paying . <p> * Fritz is an international and human rights legal expert , working in civil society .

@@123029199 <h> DOCUMENTS <h> ANC reaffirms its progressive internationalism <p> Party reaffirms its support for the genocide case against Israel at the ICJ <p> ANC reaffirms its progressive internationalism in service of South Africa 's developmental agenda <p> 31 March 2025 <p> The African National Congress ( ANC ) reaffirms its steadfast commitment to a foreign policy that serves the people of South Africa and advances a more just , peaceful , equitable and inclusive world . As the governing party , the ANC views international relations as a strategic lever to advance the national democratic revolution , stimulate inclusive economic development , deepen solidarity with oppressed people , and build a better Africa and a better world . <p> During the ANC National Executive Committee ( NEC ) meeting , the NEC received a comprehensive report from its International Relations Sub-Committee . The discussion , which took place on the first day of the NEC meeting , focused on a contemporary scan of global dynamics , strategic challenges and emerging opportunities . The deliberations reflected the vibrant battle of ideas that defines the ANC , and were deeply rooted in @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Our Values , Anchored in the Freedom Charter <p> The ANC 's international relations are not transactional -- they are transformational . Since its formation in 1912 , the ANC has advanced a values-based foreign policy . These values were reaffirmed in the Freedom Charter , which boldly declared : " There shall be Peace and Friendship ! " . This continues to guide our work to this day . <p> --&gt; <p> Our foreign policy is grounded in the pillars of Pan-Africanism , anti-imperialism , international solidarity , respect for international law and human rights , non-alignment , multilateralism , and the peaceful resolution of conflict . We reject neo-colonialism , unilateralism , and all forms of domination that undermines the sovereignty and dignity of all nations . <p> These principles are operationalised through the ANC 's five strategic pillars : building a better Africa and world ; party-to-party and multilateral relations ; transformation of global governance ; policy development ; and global solidarity campaigns . <p> Building a better Africa and better world <p> The NEC discussions premised on the recent ANC Lekgotla , held in January 2025 @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ a number of priority areas for the advancement of the African Agenda 2063 and the building of a Better World . The ANC recognises that the implementation of ANC foreign policy priorities take place within a very challenging global environment as a result of the rapidly changing balance of forces and the ensuing challenges that confront South Africa . <p> --&gt; <p> In ensuring a better Africa , there has been commendable developments towards the silencing of the guns in line with Agenda 2063 . The ANC reiterated its dedication to peaceful resolutions in conflict-affected areas , including Cabo del Gado , South Sudan , Sudan , Eritrea , Somalia , Tigray in Ethiopia , Eastern DRC , and the Sahel . It expressed concern over escalating tensions in the Horn of Africa and remains committed to ongoing peace-building processes . <p> We welcome the work of the SADC , in the Eastern DRC , which South Africa has been part of , and we believe there should be ongoing diplomatic engagement to find lasting peaceful solutions . The ANC will continue working with all partners within the region and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ noted with concern the resurgence and spreading of right-wing ideology and its adverse impact in international politics and in particular the African continent . This poses a serious risk to the global South and undermines global solidarity efforts and gives rise to the imposition of unilateralism in the international system . <p> These right-wing tendencies weaken the principle and practice of the sovereign equality of states . <p> --&gt; <p> Southern Africa faces challenges including terrorism in northern Mozambique , economic sanctions in Zimbabwe , and political instability in eSwatini , underscoring the need for regional solidarity and collective intervention . <p> Our strategic agreements with the European Union in areas of science , technology , climate innovation , and decarbonisation have created new avenues for local industrialisation and green jobs . These engagements are unlocking investment , supporting the beneficiation of local minerals , and ensuring that the shift to a low-carbon economy is pursued in a way that protects the livelihoods and developmental interests of the majority . <p> We note the global shifts from unipolarity to multipolarity , driven by the emergence of powers like China and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ cooperation . Rising unilateralism and the erosion of multilateral governance institutions require renewed commitment to a fair , just , and inclusive multipolar world order . We further note the litany of the executive orders from the US administration , including the return of our ambassador . Despite this , we urge our government to continue to pursue diplomatic engagements with the US government . <p> The ANC condemns in the strongest terms the ongoing campaign of misinformation by local right-wing elements , who seek to distort reality , incite racial tensions , and falsely claim a so-called " white genocide " in South Africa . This dangerous and racially charged rhetoric not only undermines our democracy but seeks to reverse the hard-won gains of a free , united , and non-racial society . <p> We reject their divisive politics and call on them instead to join hands with the rest of the country to build a better South Africa -- one where the economic exclusion of the black majority is decisively addressed , and all who live in it work together to shape a shared , inclusive , united @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ key initiative towards a more equitable global economic and political system , challenging established institutions and norms dominated and imposed by Western powers . Our role within BRICS and G20 promotes solidarity , equality , sustainability , equitable development , and cooperation <p> The ANC will prioritise solidarity campaigns supporting Western Sahara , Cuba , and Palestine , reinforcing its stance against imperialism and colonialism . The ANC supports the South African Government 's efforts to advocate for the Palestinian people including the case at ICJ against Israel 's genocidal activities in Gaza whose victims are mainly women and children , calling for a ceasefire and humanitarian corridors for aid .
